ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e17141	I can remind you once again – you may have seen it at the time of your arrival – that we have prepared the first SDGs here and that scientists and researchers also provide them to you. In 2015, the United Nations adopted the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development. All 193 Member States have committed themselves to implementing the 17 sustainable development objectives contained therein, which we briefly call SDGs to work at regional, national and international level. A group of committed parliamentarians has been found in the Austrian Parliament, especially for the ladies and gentlemen in front of the television equipment, in order to work politically on these objectives over the last year, and to bring them all closer to the individual goals in the course of the parliamentary sessions. It should also be underlined that national parliaments are actively involved in the implementation of these objectives, the international objectives, in their democratic role as a representative, controlling and legislative body. The beginning makes the group a goal of 14 – life under water today. In the Kleine Redoutensaal you will find today for the first time the built-in information stand – we will of course extend this to the other objectives at the next meetings. Point First reading: People’s desire to “implement unconditional basic income! ” (1628 e.g.)
ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e28562	We're on the 5th. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. Magnificence comes to the fore. Ernst Gödl. - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e28592	Mr President. Dear ladies and gentlemen! High house! The popular desire to implement unconditional basic income! Give us once again the excellent opportunity to debate the function and nature of our social state. Obviously, the initiators have followed the principle that it would be better to change our previous social system, in such a way that each person reported in Austria receives a certain amount without any condition, without any consideration, and the month after month. The appeal for popular desire was not very great, it signed about 169,000 people, 2.66 percent. But the threshold has been reached that we must debate this here in the House. It is good to have a proper debate on the principles of our social state. The fact that the appeal for this popular desire was not so great may also have good reasons. On the one hand, this certainly has economic reasons: If one wanted to introduce a basic income, of course it would have to be existential. At present, we have set an amount with the need-oriented minimum guarantee and with the social support in our social state, which is intended to enable this provision of living. It is currently around 977 euros. In order to be able to calculate this perhaps easier, the unconditional basic income should amount to EUR 1 000 per person. What would that mean purely economic? So if all around nine million people living in Austria were transferred a monthly sum of EUR 1,000, that would mean EUR 108 billion in expenditure for the State in the year. Do you know how high the tax revenue in Austria was in 2021? – They amounted to about 114 billion euros. It is clear from this that if one wanted to implement the principle of unconditional basic income, one would have to ask for much, much higher taxes from those who pay taxes, i.e. those who have a job that goes to work, who receive a wage, from the entrepreneurs and also from the farmers. This model is therefore highly questionable for economic reasons. But I think we should look deeper: In my view, there are also social policy reasons why unconditional basic income can never lead to a positive result. Is an unconditional basic income really an expression of a fair society? Is a unconditional basic income actually in the sense of human dignity? – From our point of view, I would like to say: No, no, no, no, As a People's Party, we have defined our guiding principle – the Christian-humanist image – in our party programme, and there are a few basic principles: Responsibility and ownership, security, freedom, solidarity, subsidiarity. But solidarity, ladies and gentlemen, must never be a one-way street in a society. But the unconditional basic income would be such a one-way road. Rather, we understand our social state in such a way that the one who is able to work, who can provide a service, must also provide a service in order to finance himself, on the one hand, in order to finance his family, but on the other hand, to contribute in the form of tax contributions to all the tasks of the State – for the social state, for the infrastructure, for the education system, for security and the like. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Kollross.]] That is, a solidar system can never be a one-way road. Austria is actually a very well developed social state. Those who need support get them. The person who loses his job is entitled to unemployment benefit, emergency aid or minimum security. [[Abg. Lindner: They pay for that!]] We support families in the form of family allowances or students in the form of the study allowance and the like. So there are many social security measures to ensure that every person in Austria is protected. The unconditional basic income would be contrary to this good social state, rather than endangering it. There are already some experiences from other countries in Europe. In Switzerland, about five years ago, there was an initiative with a referendum at the end, where the Swiss – with three-quarters majority – clearly stated that they rejected such a model of basic security. There were also pilot projects in Finland and Canada, and these projects were also broken down for good reason. Ladies and gentlemen, we will of course discuss this popular desire properly – it is also good to question our social state again and again – but so little As has been said in the population, it is likely to be maintained here in Parliament too.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e28639	Next speaker is Mag.a Verena Nussbaum. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e28663	Mr President. High house! Dear spectators at the gallery! This popular desire for unconditional basic income does not specify the amount of funding or the amount to be paid. These are, however, the key points of an unconditional basic income: How high is the monthly payment that each and every individual receives, and how is it financed? In the case of unconditional basic income, it is also a question of paying so-called unpaid work. In Austria, however, it is still the case that gender roles are very strongly arrested on the subject of unpaid work and are largely done by women. We would see this in such a way that an unconditional basic income would further consolidate these gender roles and not lead to a more just distribution of unpaid work. In the social state, as we know it in Austria, everyone and everyone gets what he or she needs. As a social state, we finance access to kindergartens, schools, health care, care and much more. In the case of unconditional basic income, on the other hand, everyone gets the same amount, regardless of their personal needs, the money is distributed with the pot. Now for financing: There are always different numbers throughout the room, Mr Gödl has spoken of 1 000 euros a month. I've got it. read, a monthly amount of 1,200 euros would be effective against the risk of poverty and would cost 110 billion euros per year. That is about as much as our social state costs us – but that would give us zero room for other important improvements, regardless of how the financing of these additional 110 billion would be achieved. We see our social state in a good condition, but it is quite capable of expanding, and we do not have money, for example, for the increase of care capacities or for the creation of additional childcare facilities. However, the concept is not reliable for those who depend on unconditional basic income. It is uncertain because social state benefits are better protected politically and legally from cuts. As an example, I call the social security fund: Contributions are being paid on an ongoing basis, so that one also acquires entitlements to benefits. For years acquired claims, such as the pension claim, are particularly protected by law, which the Constitutional Court has also often held – the policy cannot simply shorten as it pleases. [[It is a pleasure to see the SPÖ and the Deputies of Disoski and Koza.]] In the case of unconditional basic income, however, this would be quite different: Since it would be the same for all, no one would have a right to a special benefit, and even cuts would be much easier to make. I believe that we should further develop the social state that our ancestors and we have built together, especially in the care and free kindergartens. I still see the social state as the best way to improve people's lives in Austria. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] In addition, however, there are many screws where we can turn to achieve a more socially just society, above all a capital tax and finally a reduction in working time with full pay compensation. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e28732	The next speaker is Mr Mag. Christian Ragger. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e28757	Mr President. Dear ladies and gentlemen of this House! I will now try to explain in a compressed form why we are looking at this popular desire very critically and will therefore also reject it. First of all, as my previous speakers have mentioned today, is the financialability: It is almost impossible to finance an unconditional basic income in Austria, because the cost would be EUR 108 billion. If we face this with our revenue – there are still 60 billion social security contributions, which Mr Gödl has forgotten; these too would be taken into account there – financial viability is already obsolete. The second point is even more interesting: At the time, I was involved when we introduced the minimum guarantee, and the minimum guarantee has caused us to level down in our service company. The difference between those who work and contribute to the state with their activities – this is also a civil obligation; a certain range of services – and those who get minimum security – that should be the last social care network – is no longer true. This is a very essential thing today: We all have to work to ensure that performance is worth it again! I can't give everyone – forgive me for saying so – that's why they just give 1 000 euros a month, without I get a performance as a state! So that's the second point. The third point: If we are discussing this popular desire here, we must be clear that this will not go at all without a referendum, because we are talking about an overall change in the federal constitution! Minimum security is national competence, many people with disabilities are national competences, and pensions are Federal competence. That is to say, all the changes related to unconditional basic income in these areas must be subject to a referendum, and we must be clear here in the House. It is also a question of European law that this concerns obstacles and restrictions. If we risk a look at Germany: There is a discussion about Hartz IV, and now Greens, Liberals and SPD are discussing an unconditional basic income, but they are already encountering constitutional and European barriers. In summary, then, these three points can be said: An unconditional basic income in Austria is difficult to understand and impossible to implement. Thank you.

In the debate that took place at the time when the two government parties adopted the CO2 tax, this was also a central element of the arguments: that a price signal must be found so that people change their heating systems, their mobility and all the other areas that can be influenced. But we must look at reality and say: The price signal is currently so strong that you really don't have to put a CO2 tax on top of it at the moment, whether it's big or small. Finally, the meaning of a CO2 tax is not to burden people more, but to actually create a transformation in order to create a climate-neutral life and economy in the household, at work, in the private and in the professional environment. ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e31980	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister for Finance! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! High house! I would like to start by thank you very much for the discussion on the content of the federal accounts for 2021. This federal accounts are now available to you as a comprehensive index on the financial situation of the federal government, in time for the decision to make the new budget. We discussed the federal accounts with Mr Finanzminister yesterday in the Committee on Budgets and he is already on the agenda of the National Council. Allow me, once again, to repeat the results presented! The year 2021 was marked by the Covid Pandemic, and the measures to combat this pandemic, which were set up, were just the result of the federal budget's 2021 budget. The net result at minus EUR 19.6 billion was still very negative. The net financial balance was minus EUR 17.9 billion. Net assets have deteriorated by 18 billion euros, and the state of the adjusted financial debt of the federal government amounted to 253,567 billion euros. It was EUR 15.595 billion higher than in 2020. Overall, we achieved a public deficit of minus 5.9% of GDP. The general government debt, including the countries involved, totalled 334 billion euros and remained high, in particular through further debt collection to finance the crisis management measures. ==References====External links== It is worth mentioning in relation to the 2021 financial result, that while the revenue on the previous year increased significantly by €9.3 billion, the expenditure of the economy also increased by €5.3 billion. The high costs resulted, in particular, from the continuing measures to deal with the pandemic, such as the aid measures of Cofag-Kurzwerk et cetera, and from measures in the health field. These were tests, these were replacements according to the Epidemic Act and the Covid-19 Equivalency Subsidy Act. And, of course, the Federal contribution to pensions and grants to the ÖBB infrastructure should also be mentioned. Ladies and gentlemen! For some time now, we have heard from economic researchers that the future economic development is characterised by major uncertainties, in particular due to inflation development, the global energy crisis and the situation on the labour market, which is currently characterised by an imbalance between supply and demand. It follows that unlike in the past, we cannot rely solely on the economy to meet the budgetary challenges for the next few years. We have taken on a high debt to deal with the pandemic. This has also been possible because the interest rate has fallen continuously. In absolute terms, however, pre-taxes for interest are already EUR 51.6 billion. These are commitments due to future financial years, which, of course, in the case of other financial conditions can also have a poor impact on the result. So you don't misunderstand me. The Court of Auditors has also expressly expressed its support for aid in the management of pandemics, because the state must support it in the crisis. This also applies to security of supply in times of inflation and energy crisis. However, the Court of Auditors also advises at this point that support must always be targeted, effective and temporary, otherwise we will charge public finances for a fee. [[Congratulations on Members of the NEOS.]] We also remind ourselves that we must not forget the structural reform needs. Many areas where structural reforms are needed have long been known to us: Care, education, pensions, health et cetera. New areas include energy, infrastructure, digitisation, housing and security. They require that new solutions be identified, and the public authorities are required to do so. In the medium-term budget planning, it is about praising existing risks due to global uncertainty as well as structural reforms so that we can pursue a budget path for the next few years, which does not exclude manoeuvring opportunities for the future. I would like to stress once again: It is undisputed that there is an acute need for action in the crisis to support particularly affected areas and to support particularly affected groups of people. On the other hand, public households are also burdened and must always remain planable and predictable. Accordingly, we have a focus of testing. Our testing focus is “Next Generation Austria – Are we leaving the next generation more than debt? For the future role of the state for the next generation". From the point of view of the Court of Auditors, it is his task to control the transparency, the effectiveness and the perceptibility of the use of public funds. If the state is expanding, there is also a need for strong control. There are already many funding operations in the Covid area Lessons learned how to deal with these support measures. In the course of the audit of the financial statements, we have also made some recommendations, for example in connection with liquidity management, with trustworthiness, with transparency in the calculation of work performance and consulting services. As has been said, we are concerned with transparency and completeness. Our federal accounts should be a basis for your further work. - Thank you.

We can learn from it our lessons for the future. However, it is always very easy to look at the pandemic and the government's support measures. In total, and this is also confirmed by the economic researchers, the experts, not only in Austria but also internationally, our economic aid has been an essential reason why we have come out of the crisis so well economically. After the pandemic, growth rates of 5.3 percent were confirmed. We were thus far above the European cut, far above our neighbours, and this also shows how well economic aid finally worked. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] This, of course, also gives us a certain upholstery, certain possibilities that we need right now, in these difficult times – a challenging autumn and winter come upon us – in order to balance the costs accordingly. Even in the process, I think, one should take a certain differentiated look at the whole. A lot of things that Cofag has done was sensible and targeted, and something the legislator has sharpened during the pandemic because it has not proved to be so efficient. That's good, you have to learn from such mistakes, of course, but also there is: After the crisis, in the reflection, an assessment is always much easier than in the meantime when it has to go quickly and one has to act urgently. During the pandemic, everyone calls for more speed, for more help, for faster and as generous as possible, and that is also in the nature of the thing. To show the dimensions of what the Cofag did: More than 1.3 million grants of 14 billion euros have been paid by Cofag and paid to Austrian companies. I have a great understanding that it may be too slow for some companies, but again: 1.3 million grants paid! In peak periods, more than 100,000 applications were processed per month, which is already a huge achievement, and for this the employees there also pay great respect. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and MEPs of the Greens.]] It is also clear that, in the case of such large amounts that we are now talking about, there is also an urgent need for appropriate transparency about the payments. We in the Federal Government, but also those responsible in the countries and in the municipalities, operate with taxpayers' money, and the taxpayers of course have a right to know how their funds are used. As regards the state aid measures, there is therefore also a legal reporting obligation to Parliament. We did so again yesterday in the Committee on Budgets and we have three reports. The Ministry of Finance is debated, including comprehensive data on economic aid. So far, reporting has affected only very specific aid measures – now we have increased this transparency in the course of a change in the transparency database law and extended it to all the assistance paid through the non-profit organisations support funds. Such personal surveys, which the colleague of the Greens has already mentioned, were so far only possible in the EU's aid transparency database, and aid over EUR 100 000 had to be registered there. We are now taking a step forward in Austria by publishing all the beneficiaries who receive at least 10 000 euros per year via the Cofag or the AMA. So, in total, there is more public transparency, which is important, and we are increasing transparency in these promotions. We were always important that we deal with taxpayers as transparently as possible, so we are now creating this legal basis so that everyone can get information about the use of the aid funds. Behind these amounts we will publish in the future, but of course also stand for economic existences and personal existences. As I said, I am open to any criticism – which we should avoid, however, is a debate of envy on the backs of workers and on the backs of entrepreneurs. Thank you.

The published data shall be updated monthly and shall be displayed by the end of 2025 at the latest. In parallel, the NPO Fund Act is also amended, since future Fund payments are to be published as early as EUR 1,500 per year. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Love Seers and Watch the gallery and in front of the screens! The war of Russia's attack against Ukraine triggered a mass refugee movement into the European Union, including the federal territory of Austria. Austria is aware of its humanitarian responsibility, showed itself from the beginning and still shows solidarity. Currently, displaced people from Ukraine have an ex plainly effective temporary right of residence and immediate access to the labour market. This means they acquire immediate and collective temporary protection without having to perform an individual test. Their limited stay in Austria serves to bridge the acute risk situation and is aimed at returning to Ukraine as soon as possible. The displaced are a special group whose exceptional need for help requires special, mostly short-term financial support, and children who are currently displaced must be particularly protected and supported. [[Acceptance with the ÖVP and the Abg. Neßler.]] For this reason, people who have been expelled in Ukraine due to the military actions and find temporary protection in Austria receive Austrian family benefits for their children – of course, if they meet the required requirements. As early as July, we decided to grant the children mentioned above the right to family allowances. So it is only consistent from my point of view that we are now also the one from the 12th century onwards. On March 20, 2022, a retroactive claim to child care benefits was made. Ladies and gentlemen, what we are doing here is living European solidarity for displaced people from Ukraine. Families and children from Ukraine now receive support for a certain period of time. I am proud that even in my home region, the southeastern Styria, displaced families – in particular women and children – found reception. I feel that the willingness to help does not tear off. People support displaced people in different areas: Be it by collecting donations for them, whether through inclusion and integration in the associations, through active assistance from public authorities or through special efforts to integrate them into educational institutions. Here too – I would like to emphasize this in particular – our educators and educators do excellent work. [[Acceptance with the ÖVP and the Abg. Ribo.]] Thank you, anyway, to each individual and every individual for active engagement for our fellow human beings from Ukraine, and thank you for making your contribution to alleviate the suffering of the displaced!

ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e34956	Madam President. Minister! High house! ==References== February of this year Russia has attacked Ukraine illegally, triggering a mass flight movement that had incredible dimensions. Millions were and are on the run. The EU activated the so-called mass inflow directive, which was created in 2001 in response to the inflow of civilian refugees during the Yugoslavian war. This directive, which we all hoped for, was indeed convinced that we would never need it, because it was unimaginable for us to have a war of attack again on European soil, lays down minimum standards for granting temporary protection in the event of mass flight. However, it is also intended to bring with it measures to ensure a balanced distribution of the burden through the inclusion of persons. Ladies and gentlemen, this point will have to be considered in due course, because the performance of countries such as Poland, for example, as a direct neighboring country, which has taken up hundreds of thousands and millions of refugees, has probably only been compensated for. Austria, too, showed and shows itself in solidarity and has received nearly 80 000 displaced Ukrainians. Mostly women, mothers and their children, find protection in Austria. The relationship of family benefits is an extremely important step for these people. But since displaced people from Ukraine have their own EU legal system These persons were not entitled to family benefits in Austria. After we have settled family allowances retroactively by mid-March before the summer, this will now also be carried out legally in respect of the childcare allowance. These decisions are also the prerequisite for other family benefits, such as the free movement of students and the various benefits of the countries. All of this, Mr Bernhard, we have also made constitutional reform – that is why the long discussion has been held. That was a real challenge, and that's why it moved. Ladies and gentlemen, each and every one of us probably knows someone who had to flee Ukraine. We know whether we can imagine that these people, with their thoughts in their home country and with their hearts, are with their loved ones and relatives in Ukraine. With these decisions, we are at least taking the material concern of these people in our country. I would like to express my thanks to all of you for your approval.

The effectiveness of these Court audits is important and crucial: Of 321 recommendations, 94 were implemented, 61 were partially implemented, 132 were implemented and 54 were not implemented. This shows, however, that there were quite positive shifts – from 79.9% to 84.2 percent in 2021. The Court of Auditors is constantly thinking for the future. There are certainly things that run out, but there are also new challenges: The project or the audit focus for the next few years, Next Generation Austria, is in view. It is about the fact that we can no longer leave the future generations behind more debt than is necessary and the state can be further developed forward-looking and sustainable. I think this is a very exciting examination, because we need to invest a lot of money in crisis management at the moment: What can we afford, and how far can we go? In this sense, I believe it is important for the Court of Auditors to examine cost-intensive reform projects such as digitisation, administration, anti-corruption and reform in the field of education, climate change and pensions. At present – and we will be returning to our old-fashioned Parliament in the foreseeable future – there is a commission by the House's Building Committee, after which the review of Parliament's renewal – with the BIG has already begun – is under way. It will simply be about – there are cost increases as well – that one questions the procurement procedures and the cost effects due to delays in construction. There are currently 72 tests and seven of them are special tests. Finally, you can say: In the future, it is important that follow-up checks and demand procedures, which exist again and again, are put under the motto – and that the corresponding challenge is met –: Could the problem be solved? Is the problem solved? In this sense, I would like to thank the Court of Auditors once again. There are now, I think, very big challenges. It is also about resources for the next time. There are also wishes from the Court of Auditors as regards personnel matters. We will try in the budget to equip the Court of Auditors in such a way that good and sustainable audit work is possible. Thank you.

The Austrian Court of Auditors is one of those accounting courts that focus on economic auditing. So it's not just about financial audits, it's about financial audits. The Court of Auditors has reviewed the Federal Reserve System from November 2018 to March 2019. The budget reserves in the Federal Ministry of Finance, the Federal Ministry of Labour, Social Affairs and Consumer Protection as well as the Federal Ministry of Digitalization and Economic Location have been reviewed. The purpose of the review was to review the development of the deposit system, in particular the process of withdrawal, efficiency and effectiveness. The period from 2009 to 2017 has been verified. I would like to mention that in 2009 and 2013 there has been a two-stage budgetary reform which has fundamentally changed the system. What does that mean? – This budgetary reserve system works in such a way that, if the money of a place has not been used in a financial year, the Ministry of Finance has, under certain circumstances, been able to transform this money into reserves. The advantage is that the resort was able to retrieve this money later and has continued to have access to it. The difference to prior to 2009 is also that this money has not been financed until when it has been needed. Mr Krainer has already mentioned a stand today: In 2016, for example, this amounted to more than EUR 20.6 billion, with the report stressing the decline in reserves in 2012 and 2017. It can be imagined that this has often led to problems when such reserves have been raised by the resort at the moment – this is also reflected in the report – because in the course of the period the funding was a little difficult to draw up. In my community there is the community proposal, in the federal government there is the federal proposal, and it is governed by the Federal Finance Act. If a resort needs money for the next year, it must be depicted somewhere. The Court of Auditors has also recommended a change as there have been a number of problems in this regard in the past: There have been cash flows, in which it was already predictable that you need them, but they were almost not shown in the budget. The first recommendation was to change that. If unforeseen events have occurred, the Ministry of Finance has, of course, been able to decide on these flows. This has been done by the Ministry of Finance and not here in the National Council on the legislation. The second recommendation concerns the duration, the period during which such withdrawal may have taken place. It can be imagined that if a place of business has urgently needed this money, it is not so flexible and one has no planning certainty as longer deadlines are to be observed. The third recommendation is to start a reform. We have already heard it at the Committee meeting, and it is very positive that our Minister for Finance, Magnus Brunner, is planning to start a reform. According to his statement, we may experience some sort of result this year. I would like to thank the Court of Auditors at this point and stress the importance of this control body. In fact, this control body is also a driving force for new reforms, and I believe that this is very important for the further development of our Republic of Austria. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e38111	Mr President. Madam President. Dear High House! Dear Seers! I would like to refer briefly to item 21 of the agenda, namely the report on the federal budget reserves. The Court of Auditors has reviewed the Federal Reserve System, in particular with regard to reserves of the Ministry of Labour and Economic Affairs for a period from 2009 to 2017. Since the reform of the reserve system in the context of the last budgetary reform, it is true that ministries that do not consume all the budget appropriations that are actually estimated can form reserves in the Ministry of Finance, which they can then access later, for example in the following year, with the agreement of the Ministry of Finance. This regulation is particularly designed to ensure that ministries do not have the pressure to spend the whole money in December, but to be able to use what remains, for example, in the next or the next year. The departments must apply to the BMF if they want to dissolve the deposit, and the Court of Auditors has found that the processing time in the Ministry of Finance is, in such a request, relatively different, depending on which ministry has applied for the resolution of a deposit. You can also assign this to a little bit of party colors if you are researched. For example, on average, the Ministry of Economic Affairs has waited 61 days for approval of the Ministry of Finance. At the Ministry of Transport it was 109 days, almost twice as many. It is particularly interesting – and this has also been pointed out – that one sees that the waiting period varies greatly in the area of processing in the Ministry of Finance's political cabinet, which, of course, more strongly suggests that there may have been political reasons for these differences. This is in addition to two other points which I would like to mention, so to speak, an important part which, I believe, would have to be reformed in the reserve system if there were to be a possible budgetary reform. The two other points which constantly criticise both the Court of Auditors and the budgetary service as well as an external evaluation are that the reserves are neither limited in the amount nor in terms of the period during which they should be dissolved. I believe that this is an occasion – and this is what the report gives a little motivation for, that you really look at the deposit system again. Thank you.

With regard to the scope of data, data on testing, clustering and segregation processes are needed, but also data for the utilization of hospitals and the established area. It is about the quality of the data and the degree of detail. It's about completeness. It is about the linkability of data – of course all of this is in the field of data protection – and it is about electronic possibilities in the collection methodology – which should then be facilitated as well, It is a matter of coordinated communication. Accordingly, we welcome the fact that there have been better coordinated publications of corona data since last week. In the case of resources, we can also draw lessons from the crisis regarding the procurement of protective equipment and medical goods. There would be a need to develop crisis and disaster plans. The crisis hotline 1450 would also be improved. It has not been created as a crisis hotline, but as a health advice agency. With regard to contact tracking, a monitoring and control process would be established. In the case of the health system in general, in the event of a crisis, we express our support for an information and cooperation obligation of health insurance providers, hospitals and public health institutions, especially in the event of a crisis and disaster. As far as pandemic management is concerned, there were, of course, mistakes that were already in the past: that the pandemic plan was not up-to-date and was not updated – and was not made after there were risk reports – and that the epidemic law was outdated. We are also pleased when recommendations are taken up. It is a legal framework for a crisis mechanism with clear processes and responsibilities, including communication. Of course, the Ministry of Health has the leading role in the field of health. And it is about the functionality of the public health structures, which naturally need to be set up in a human and continuous manner. As far as medical education is concerned, we have different interfaces: There are on the one hand the medical universities, there is the clinical-practical year and then there is the medical education. It is important that all areas are coordinated. As far as the graduates are concerned, there was a 31 percent drop-out quota in the follow-up to the medical research. This was compensated a little by draw. There are discussions in the field of general medicine. It is true that the number of doctors in Austria increased by 23 percent from 2009 to 2020, but only by 8 percent in the field of general medicine. The central part of the reform, which was already in place seven years ago, was actually to improve general medical training and teaching practice in the established area. In this respect, the objectives have not yet been achieved. There was also a trend in changing back to a specialist training. There are potential for improvement. The training of a specialist General medicine is now a subject on which one seems to have agreed. However, the problem also exists in the field of demand assessment. In fact, it is not only about the number of estimated retirements, but in our opinion it is also about other factors that influence the “medical needs” – under quotation marks: Part-time, new supply models, primary supply facilities, opening hours, demand for medical services or demographic developments. All of this would have to be incorporated into a needs analysis. That would be the reports. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-09-21-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00171_d8e39369	Mr President. Madam President of the Court of Auditors! Dear ladies and gentlemen! High house! The Court of Auditors has carried out a follow-up review on the procurement of medicinal products in Austria, and it has shown that there are problems of supply worldwide, Europe-wide, but also in Austria. This, of course, means that patient care is potentially at risk and that it is also costing the hospitals. This was explicitly examined at the Salzburger Landesapotheke and at the Landeskrankenhaus Innsbruck. This follow-up review has once again shown some similarities: There are also difficulties in delivery – especially in substitutes or generic medicines, for example in the field of antibiotics. What are the reasons for this? – As a result of increasing globalisation, there are, of course, fewer and fewer providers for medicinal products. There is a fall in drug prices due to cost pressure and parallel exports. All in all, the regulation was adopted by the Minister of Health, who took place on 1 January 1989. In April 2020, a number of measures concerning the supply of medicinal products have entered into force with regard to parallel exports, reporting obligations and much more. In conclusion, I think, one important thing: We in Austria need a secure supply in the future. The Federal Office for Safety in Health, Basg, has worked out a lot in a task force and will implement these proposals in the future. It has already been reported today that there are three areas in the procurement of medicinal products that present challenges for the future: first, the prescription of active substances, second, compliance with procurement regulations and thirdly, the assessment bodies for extractors.
